<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a fully polynomial randomized approximation scheme for counting the common bases of two arbitrary matroids of the same rank, supplied by independence oracles. The algorithm requires no explicit representation of either matroid and has polynomial bounds on both oracle calls and bit operations on every execution.

- **claim-common-bases-fpras** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a single randomized oracle algorithm with the following guarantee. Given independence oracles for arbitrary rank-$r$ matroids $M_1,M_2$ on $[n]$, and rational $\epsilon,\delta\in(0,1)$, it outputs a nonnegative rational $\widehat Z$ such that $$\mathbb P\bigl[(1-\epsilon)Z(M_1,M_2)\le\widehat Z \le(1+\epsilon)Z(M_1,M_2)\bigr]\ge1-\delta.$$ If $Z(M_1,M_2)=0$, its output is always zero. On every execution, the number of oracle calls and the number of other bit operations are bounded by a fixed polynomial in $$n,\qquad \ell,\qquad \epsilon^{-1},\qquad \log\delta^{-1},$$ where $\ell$ is the binary encoding length of $n,r,\epsilon,\delta$. The algorithm uses only independent unbiased random bits and does not require a representation of either matroid.
  - Explanation: Counts common bases of arbitrary equal-rank matroids through independence oracles, with relative-error and confidence guarantees and polynomial oracle and bit work on every execution. Exact emptiness testing makes the output identically zero on infeasible inputs.
- **claim-lorentzian-rank-polynomial** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-bh)): For a matroid $N$ on a ground set $E$ and $0<q\le1$, the polynomial $$T_{q,N}(z_0,z)=\sum_{A\subseteq E} q^{-\operatorname{rk}_N(A)}z_0^{\|E\|-\|A\|}\prod_{a\in A}z_a$$ is Lorentzian. Lorentzian polynomials are closed under partial derivatives and nonnegative linear substitutions, and a quadratic Lorentzian polynomial has a Hessian with at most one positive eigenvalue. In particular, $T_{q,N}$ has the quadratic signature property.
  - Explanation: The matroid rank polynomial is Lorentzian. Closure under differentiation and nonnegative substitutions supplies the quadratic signature property used for both selected-element and omitted-element polynomials.
- **claim-conditional-defect-coefficients** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-coefficient)): For distinct unassigned indices $i,j$, $$\label{eq:coefficientpair} 4c^{\sigma}_{ij}c^{\sigma}_{ji}\le z_{\sigma}^2.$$ For distinct unassigned indices $i,j,k$, $$\label{eq:coefficient} c^{\sigma}_{ij}c^{\sigma}_{jk}\le z_{\sigma}c^{\sigma}_{ik}.$$
  - Explanation: Controls products of conditional defect totals using transversal totals and another defect total. The inequalities remain available after a partial transversal assignment.
- **claim-missing-slot-transport** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transport)): With the notation above, $$\label{eq:transport} (\overline H_p-\overline H_d)^2 \le \left(\frac1{s_pW_p}+\frac1{s_dW_d} +\frac2{s_ps_d}\sum_{t=1}^m\frac{v_t}{W_t}\right) D_{\mathcal X}(H).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the difference of weighted means for the missing-singleton classes by exchange energy. Its coefficient includes endpoint masses and the totals associated with doubled ordinary slots.
- **claim-transversal-variance** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transversalvariance)): For good multipliers and every $H:\Omega\to\mathbb R$, $$\label{eq:transversalvariance} \operatorname{Var}_\mu H\le C_{\rm p}\frac{D(H)}{c_0}, \qquad C_{\rm p}=n(1+8n).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds all variation on individual transversals by the enlarged exchange energy. Conditional coefficient inequalities select a branching pair, and variance decomposition sums the resulting transport bounds.
- **claim-defect-mean-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-defecttransfer)): Assume the multipliers are good, and let $H:\Omega\to\mathbb R$. For each defect type $ij$ there is an event $\mathcal A\subseteq\Omega_0$ obtained by prescribing the selected elements in pairs $i,j$, with $\mu(\mathcal A)\ge1/4$. Its conditional mean $\overline H'=\mathbb E_\mu[H\mid\mathcal A]$ satisfies $$\label{eq:defecttransfer} (\overline H_{ij}-\overline H')^2 \le C_{\rm d}\frac{D(H)}{c_0}, \qquad C_{\rm d}=8+32n,$$ and $$\label{eq:conditionalmean} (\overline H'-\overline H_0)^2\le4\operatorname{Var}_\mu H.$$
  - Explanation: Connects each defect-class mean to the mean of a sufficiently frequent transversal event. A separate conditional-mean estimate then connects that event to the full transversal mean.
- **claim-observable-poincare** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-observable)): For good multipliers and every $H:\Omega\to\mathbb R$, $$\label{eq:observable} \operatorname{Var}_\pi(\mathsf QH)\le K_{\rm obs}\mathcal E(H), \qquad K_{\rm obs}=2000n^6.$$
  - Explanation: Controls the variance after retaining individual transversal values and averaging within each defect class. It does not assert a bound for arbitrary fluctuations inside a defect class.
- **claim-warm-start-time-averages** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-timeaverages)): Suppose the multipliers are good. If $G=\mathsf QG$ and a trajectory $(X_t)$ of $P$ starts with law $\nu\le U\pi$, then for every integer $N\ge1$, $$\label{eq:MSE} \mathbb E_\nu\left[\left(\frac1N\sum_{t=0}^{N-1}G(X_t)-\mathbb E_\pi G\right)^2\right] \le\frac{2UK_{\rm obs}\operatorname{Var}_\pi G}{N}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the mean squared error of empirical averages for observables fixed by the block-averaging projection. Pointwise warm-start domination transfers the stationary estimate to the stated starting law.
- **claim-transversal-trace-mixing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-trace)): With good multipliers, $P_A$ is reversible with stationary law $\mu$, irreducible, and lazy. Its inverse spectral gap is at most $18n^4$. In particular, for every integer $s\ge0$, $$\label{eq:tracemixing} \left\|\frac{P_A^s(x,y)}{\mu(y)}-1\right\| \le\frac{\exp(-s/(18n^4))}{\min_{z\in A}\mu(z)} \quad(x,y\in A).$$ If a trace step begins with a law $\nu\le U\mu$, its expected number of underlying transitions is at most $$\label{eq:returncost} \frac{U}{\pi(A)}\le5Un^2.$$
  - Explanation: Establishes reversibility, irreducibility, laziness, a spectral-gap bound, and a pointwise convergence estimate for successive transversal visits. The excursion-cost guarantee is an expectation under warm-start domination.
- **claim-common-independent-counting-sampling** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-cor-intersection)): Let $M_1,M_2$ be arbitrary matroids on an explicitly enumerated ground set $[n]$, supplied by fixed exact independence oracles with $n$-bit subset queries. Their ranks may differ. For an integer $k$, let $\mathcal C_k$ consist of the $k$-element subsets of $[n]$ independent in both matroids, and put $$\mathcal C=\bigcup_{j=0}^n\mathcal C_j,\qquad h=\max_{I\in\mathcal C}\|I\|,\qquad \mathcal C_{\max}=\mathcal C_h.$$ The maximum $h$ is well defined because $\varnothing\in\mathcal C$. Each family $\mathcal F\in\{\mathcal C_k,\mathcal C,\mathcal C_{\max}\}$ has the following algorithms.* 1. *Given rational $\epsilon,\delta\in(0,1)$, a randomized oracle algorithm returns a nonnegative rational $\widehat N$ satisfying $$\mathbb P\bigl[(1-\epsilon)\|\mathcal F\|\le\widehat N \le(1+\epsilon)\|\mathcal F\|\bigr]\ge1-\delta.$$ If $\mathcal F=\varnothing$, an exact pretest returns $0$ before randomization. On every execution, oracle calls and other bit operations are bounded by a fixed polynomial in $n,\ell,\epsilon^{-1},\log\delta^{-1}$.* 2. *Given rational $\theta\in(0,1)$, a randomized oracle algorithm decides emptiness exactly. If $\mathcal F=\varnothing$, it returns a distinguished symbol $\bot$. Otherwise it returns $S\in\mathcal F$ on every execution, and its output law satisfies $$d_{\rm TV}\bigl(\mathcal L(S),\operatorname{Unif}(\mathcal F)\bigr) =\frac12\sum_{I\in\mathcal F} \left\|\mathbb P[S=I]-\frac1{\|\mathcal F\|}\right\|\le\theta.$$ On every execution, oracle calls and other bit operations are bounded by a fixed polynomial in $n,\ell,\theta^{-1}$.* *Here $\ell$ is the binary encoding length of the numerical inputs to the algorithm in question, including $k$ only for $\mathcal C_k$. Both algorithms use only the two given independence oracles and independent unbiased random bits.
  - Explanation: Extends approximate counting and almost-uniform sampling to common independent sets of prescribed, unrestricted, or maximum cardinality, allowing unequal ranks. Exact branch feasibility keeps every returned sample feasible even when intermediate estimates are inaccurate.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-bh",
        "exact_quote": "For a matroid $N$ on a ground set $E$ and $0<q\\le1$, the polynomial $$T_{q,N}(z_0,z)=\\sum_{A\\subseteq E}\n        q^{-\\operatorname{rk}_N(A)}z_0^{|E|-|A|}\\prod_{a\\in A}z_a$$ is Lorentzian. Lorentzian polynomials are closed under partial derivatives and nonnegative linear substitutions, and a quadratic Lorentzian polynomial has a Hessian with at most one positive eigenvalue.         "source_id": "PM-114.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-trace",
        "exact_quote": "With good multipliers, $P_A$ is reversible with stationary law $\\mu$, irreducible, and lazy. Its inverse spectral gap is at most $18n^4$. In particular, for every integer $s\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:tracemixing}\n \\left|\\frac{P_A^s(x,y)}{\\mu(y)}-1\\right|\n \\le\\frac{\\exp(-s/(18n^4))}{\\min_{z\\in A}\\mu(z)}\n \\quad(x,y\\in A).$$ If a trace step begins with a law $\\nu\\le U\\mu$, its expected number of underlying transitions is at most $$\\label{eq:returncost}\n \\frac{U}{\\pi(A)}\\le5Un^2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-114.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-trace",
          "exact_quote": "With good multipliers, $P_A$ is reversible with stationary law $\\mu$, irreducible, and lazy. Its inverse spectral gap is at most $18n^4$.           "source_id": "PM-114.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "With good multipliers, $P_A$ is reversible with stationary law $\\mu$, irreducible, and lazy. Its inverse spectral gap is at most $18n^4$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-intersection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M_1,M_2$ be arbitrary matroids on an explicitly enumerated ground set $[n]$, supplied by fixed exact independence oracles with $n$-bit subset queries. Their ranks may differ. For an integer $k$, let $\\mathcal C_k$ consist of the $k$-element subsets of $[n]$ independent in both matroids, and put $$\\mathcal C=\\bigcup_{j=0}^n\\mathcal C_j,\\qquad\n h=\\max_{I\\in\\mathcal C}|I|,\\qquad\n \\mathcal C_{\\max}=\\mathcal C_h.$$ The maximum $h$ is well defined because $\\varnothing\\in\\mathcal C$. Each family $\\mathcal F\\in\\{\\mathcal C_k,\\mathcal C,\\mathcal C_{\\max}\\}$ has the following algorithms.*\n\n1.  *Given rational $\\epsilon,\\delta\\in(0,1)$, a randomized oracle algorithm returns a nonnegative rational $\\widehat N$ satisfying $$\\mathbb P\\bigl[(1-\\epsilon)|\\mathcal F|\\le\\widehat N\n                             \\le(1+\\epsilon)|\\mathcal F|\\bigr]\\ge1-\\delta.$$ If $\\mathcal F=\\varnothing$, an exact pretest returns $0$ before randomization. On every execution, oracle calls and other bit operations are bounded by a fixed polynomial in $n,\\ell,\\epsilon^{-1},\\log\\delta^{-1}$.*\n\n2.  *Given rational $\\theta\\in(0,1)$, a randomized oracle algorithm decides emptiness exactly. If $\\mathcal F=\\varnothing$, it returns a distinguished symbol $\\bot$. Otherwise it returns $S\\in\\mathcal F$ on every execution, and its output law satisfies $$d_{\\rm TV}\\bigl(\\mathcal L(S),\\operatorname{Unif}(\\mathcal F)\\bigr)\n     =\\frac12\\sum_{I\\in\\mathcal F}\n           \\left|\\mathbb P[S=I]-\\frac1{|\\mathcal F|}\\right|\\le\\theta.$$ On every execution, oracle calls and other bit operations are bounded by a fixed polynomial in $n,\\ell,\\theta^{-1}$.*\n\n*Here $\\ell$ is the binary encoding length of the numerical inputs to the algorithm in question, including $k$ only for $\\mathcal C_k$. Both algorithms use only the two given independence oracles and independent unbiased random bits.",
        "source_id": "PM-114.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-intersection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M_1,M_2$ be arbitrary matroids on an explicitly enumerated ground set $[n]$, supplied by fixed exact independence oracles with $n$-bit subset queries. Their ranks may differ. For an integer $k$, let $\\mathcal C_k$ consist of the $k$-element subsets of $[n]$ independent in both matroids, and put $$\\mathcal C=\\bigcup_{j=0}^n\\mathcal C_j,\\qquad\n h=\\max_{I\\in\\mathcal C}|I|,\\qquad\n \\mathcal C_{\\max}=\\mathcal C_h.$$ The maximum $h$ is well defined because $\\varnothing\\in\\mathcal C$. Each family $\\mathcal F\\in\\{\\mathcal C_k,\\mathcal C,\\mathcal C_{\\max}\\}$ has the following algorithms.*\n\n1.  *Given rational $\\epsilon,\\delta\\in(0,1)$, a randomized oracle algorithm returns a nonnegative rational $\\widehat N$ satisfying $$\\mathbb P\\bigl[(1-\\epsilon)|\\mathcal F|\\le\\widehat N\n                             \\le(1+\\epsilon)|\\mathcal F|\\bigr]\\ge1-\\delta.$$ If $\\mathcal F=\\varnothing$, an exact pretest returns $0$ before randomization. On every execution, oracle calls and other bit operations are bounded by a fixed polynomial in $n,\\ell,\\epsilon^{-1},\\log\\delta^{-1}$.*\n\n2.  *Given rational $\\theta\\in(0,1)$, a randomized oracle algorithm decides emptiness exactly. If $\\mathcal F=\\varnothing$, it returns a distinguished symbol $\\bot$. Otherwise it returns $S\\in\\mathcal F$ on every execution, and its output law satisfies $$d_{\\rm TV}\\bigl(\\mathcal L(S),\\operatorname{Unif}(\\mathcal F)\\bigr)\n     =\\frac12\\sum_{I\\in\\mathcal F}\n           \\left|\\mathbb P[S=I]-\\frac1{|\\mathcal F|}\\right|\\le\\theta.$$ On every execution, oracle calls and other bit operations are bounded by a fixed polynomial in $n,\\ell,\\theta^{-1}$.*\n\n*Here $\\ell$ is the binary encoding length of the numerical inputs to the algorithm in question, including $k$ only for $\\mathcal C_k$. Both algorithms use only the two given independence oracles and independent unbiased random bits.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Matroid** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A *matroid* $M$ on a finite set $E$ is a family $\mathcal I$ of independent sets containing $\varnothing$, closed under taking subsets, and satisfying augmentation: if $I,J\in\mathcal I$ and $\|I\|<\|J\|$, then $I\cup\{e\}\in\mathcal I$ for some $e\in J\setminus I$.
- **Rank** ([source](#pm-h-2)): The rank of $A\subseteq E$ is the largest size of an independent subset of $A$.
- **Basis** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A *basis* is a maximal independent subset of $E$; augmentation implies that all bases have size $\operatorname{rk}_M(E)$.
- **Independence oracle** ([source](#pm-h-2)): An *independence oracle* reports whether a queried subset is independent.
- **Common-basis count** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Writing $\mathcal B(M)$ for the set of bases of $M$, the quantity to be estimated is $$Z(M_1,M_2)=\|\mathcal B(M_1)\cap\mathcal B(M_2)\|.$$
- **Fully polynomial randomized approximation scheme** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A fully polynomial randomized approximation scheme, or FPRAS, achieves this guarantee with polynomial dependence on the input size, $\epsilon^{-1}$, and $\log\delta^{-1}$.
- **Dual matroid** ([source](#eq:dualrank)): The dual matroid $M^*$ has as bases the complements of the bases of $M$, and its rank is $$\label{eq:dualrank} \operatorname{rk}_{M^*}(A)=\|A\|-\operatorname{rk}_M(E)+\operatorname{rk}_M(E\setminus A).$$
- **Transversal** ([source](#sec:reduction)): A *transversal* is an $n$-set that contains exactly one element of each pair $\{x_i,y_i\}$.
- **Rank-deficiency weights** ([source](#eq:rankweights)): $$\label{eq:rankweights} d(S)=n-\operatorname{rk}_K(S),\qquad f_q(S)=q^{d(S)}.$$ These weights are strictly positive. Let $\Omega_0$ be the set of all transversals. Fixed-cardinality weights proportional to $q^{-\operatorname{rk}(S)}$ already appear in the log-concave-polynomial framework of Anari, Liu, Oveis Gharan, and Vinzant [[ALOV2024](#ref-ALOV2024), arXiv version 3, Theorem 1.6]. Here the additional restriction to paired occupancy classes is central. For distinct $i,j\in[n]$, let $\Omega_{ij}$ consist of the $n$-sets for which pair $i$ is empty, pair $j$ is full, and every other pair is singly occupied. Set $$\label{eq:types} \mathcal T=\{0\}\cup\{ij:i,j\in[n],\ i\ne j\},\qquad \Omega=\bigsqcup_{T\in\mathcal T}\Omega_T,\qquad c_T(q)=\sum_{S\in\Omega_T}f_q(S).$$ The order of the indices in $ij$ matters. In particular, $c_0(q)$ sums over all transversals, including those that are not bases. We suppress $q$ when it is fixed.
- **Ordered defect class** ([source](#sec:reduction)): For distinct $i,j\in[n]$, let $\Omega_{ij}$ consist of the $n$-sets for which pair $i$ is empty, pair $j$ is full, and every other pair is singly occupied.
- **Type totals and enlarged state space** ([source](#eq:types)): $$\label{eq:types} \mathcal T=\{0\}\cup\{ij:i,j\in[n],\ i\ne j\},\qquad \Omega=\bigsqcup_{T\in\mathcal T}\Omega_T,\qquad c_T(q)=\sum_{S\in\Omega_T}f_q(S).$$ The order of the indices in $ij$ matters. In particular, $c_0(q)$ sums over all transversals, including those that are not bases. We suppress $q$ when it is fixed.
- **Quadratic signature property** ([source](#sec:algebra)): A polynomial has the *quadratic signature property* if every homogeneous quadratic obtained from it by partial differentiation and nonnegative linear substitutions has a Hessian with at most one positive eigenvalue.
- **Partial transversal assignment** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A partial transversal assignment $\sigma$ prescribes one element in each of some pairs, requiring its inclusion and its mate’s exclusion.
- **Multiplied state weights and stationary law** ([source](#eq:chainweights)): $$\label{eq:chainweights} \lambda(S)= \begin{cases} f_q(S),&S\in\Omega_0,\\ w_{ij}f_q(S),&S\in\Omega_{ij}, \end{cases} \qquad \Lambda=\sum_{S\in\Omega}\lambda(S),\qquad \pi(S)=\frac{\lambda(S)}\Lambda.$$ The conditional law on transversals is $\mu(S)=f_q(S)/c_0$ for $S\in\Omega_0$; it does not depend on the multipliers. Call the multipliers *good* if $$\label{eq:good} \frac14w^*_{ij}\le w_{ij}\le4w^*_{ij}, \qquad w^*_{ij}=\frac{c_0}{c_{ij}}.$$ For good multipliers, $$\label{eq:typemasses} \Lambda\le5n^2c_0,\qquad \pi(\Omega_0)\ge\frac1{5n^2},\qquad \pi(\Omega_{ij})\ge\frac1{20n^2}.$$ Indeed each defect has total multiplied weight between $c_0/4$ and $4c_0$, and there are $n(n-1)$ defects.
- **Good multipliers** ([source](#eq:good)): Fix $q\in(0,1]$. For each defect type $ij$, choose a positive multiplier $w_{ij}$, and define $$\label{eq:chainweights} \lambda(S)= \begin{cases} f_q(S),&S\in\Omega_0,\\ w_{ij}f_q(S),&S\in\Omega_{ij}, \end{cases} \qquad \Lambda=\sum_{S\in\Omega}\lambda(S),\qquad \pi(S)=\frac{\lambda(S)}\Lambda.$$ The conditional law on transversals is $\mu(S)=f_q(S)/c_0$ for $S\in\Omega_0$; it does not depend on the multipliers. Call the multipliers *good* if $$\label{eq:good} \frac14w^*_{ij}\le w_{ij}\le4w^*_{ij}, \qquad w^*_{ij}=\frac{c_0}{c_{ij}}.$$ For good multipliers, $$\label{eq:typemasses} \Lambda\le5n^2c_0,\qquad \pi(\Omega_0)\ge\frac1{5n^2},\qquad \pi(\Omega_{ij})\ge\frac1{20n^2}.$$ Indeed each defect has total multiplied weight between $c_0/4$ and $4c_0$, and there are $n(n-1)$ defects.
- **Unnormalized exchange energy** ([source](#eq:D)): For $H:\Omega\to\mathbb R$, define the unnormalized energy $$\label{eq:D} D(H)=\sum_{\substack{\{S,S'\}\\S,S'\in\Omega,\ \|S\triangle S'\|=2}} \min\{\lambda(S),\lambda(S')\}\bigl(H(S)-H(S')\bigr)^2.$$ Each undirected edge is counted once. The Dirichlet form is $$\label{eq:Dirichlet} \mathcal E(H)=\langle H,(I-P)H\rangle_\pi=\frac{D(H)}{2n^2\Lambda}.$$ Here $\langle G,H\rangle_\pi=\sum_S\pi(S)G(S)H(S)$.
- **Dirichlet form** ([source](#eq:Dirichlet)): For $H:\Omega\to\mathbb R$, define the unnormalized energy $$\label{eq:D} D(H)=\sum_{\substack{\{S,S'\}\\S,S'\in\Omega,\ \|S\triangle S'\|=2}} \min\{\lambda(S),\lambda(S')\}\bigl(H(S)-H(S')\bigr)^2.$$ Each undirected edge is counted once. The Dirichlet form is $$\label{eq:Dirichlet} \mathcal E(H)=\langle H,(I-P)H\rangle_\pi=\frac{D(H)}{2n^2\Lambda}.$$ Here $\langle G,H\rangle_\pi=\sum_S\pi(S)G(S)H(S)$.
- **Block-averaging projection** ([source](#pm-h-17)): $$ The transport subgraph again uses the unnormalized conductances in $D$, so <a href="#eq:defecttransfer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:defecttransfer">[eq:defecttransfer]</a> follows. Finally, since $\mu(\mathcal A)\ge1/4$, Jensen’s inequality on $\mathcal A$ gives <a href="#eq:conditionalmean" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:conditionalmean">[eq:conditionalmean]</a>. ◻ </details> Define $\mathsf Q$ on functions on $\Omega$ by retaining every value on $\Omega_0$ and replacing the values on each defect class by that class’s conditional mean.
- **Warm starting law** ([source](#pm-h-19)): For probability distributions $\nu,\pi$ on a finite set, write $\nu\le U\pi$ to mean pointwise domination.
- **Transversal trace transition** ([source](#pm-h-20)): The *trace transition* $P_A(x,y)=\mathbb P_x(X_{\tau_A^+}=y)$ records the next visit to $A$, including a possible visit at time one.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-total-multiplied-weight | \Lambda | ≤ | $5n^2c_0$ | A fixed positive annealing parameter and good defect multipliers. |
| q-transversal-stationary-mass | \pi(\Omega_0) | ≥ | $\frac1{5n^2}$ | The transversal occupancy class at a fixed parameter. |
| q-defect-stationary-mass | \pi(\Omega_{ij}) | ≥ | $\frac1{20n^2}$ | Every existing ordered defect class. |
| q-transversal-variance-coefficient | C_{\rm p} | = | $n(1+8n)$ | The exchange-energy estimate for arbitrary functions. |
| q-defect-transfer-coefficient | C_{\rm d} | = | $8+32n$ | Every ordered defect class under good multipliers. |
| q-observable-poincare-coefficient | K_{\rm obs} | = | $2000n^6$ | Good multipliers and the specified Metropolis Dirichlet form. |
| q-trace-inverse-gap | Inverse spectral gap of the transversal trace | ≤ | $18n^4$ | A fixed annealing parameter and good multipliers. |
| q-warm-trace-excursion-cost | Expected underlying transitions in a trace step | ≤ | $5Un^2$ | The stated warmness factor. |
| q-cooling-ratio | \rho | = | $1-\frac1{2n}$ | The nonempty case after exact feasibility testing. |
| q-annealing-phase-count | L | = | $2n(n+b)$ | The accuracy parameter and the enumerated ground set. |
| q-phase-relative-tolerance | \eta | = | $\frac{\epsilon}{32(L+1)}$ | The tolerance assigned to each annealing phase. |
| q-restart-trace-length | \tau | = | $20n^4\bigl(n(L+1)+2\bigr)$ | Each stored parameter level used by a restart. |
| q-restart-transition-cap | B_{\rm restart} | = | $2000(L+1)^2n^2\tau$ | The total underlying transition attempts within one restart. |
| q-estimation-observation-count | N_{\rm av} | = | $\left\lceil\frac{10^{10}(L+1)n^{14}}{\eta^2}\right\rceil$ | The observations collected in each estimation phase. |
| q-transversal-pointwise-floor | \mu_j(S) | ≥ | $2^{-n(L+1)}$ | All scheduled phases and transversal states. |
| q-phase-average-mse | Mean squared error of each empirical average | ≤ | $\frac{40000n^8}{N_{\rm av}}$ | Type indicators and the ratio observable in the current phase. |
| q-total-analytic-failure | Probability of a restart abort or inaccurate phase | ≤ | $\frac1{16}$ | The complete exact-random-choice run. |
| q-terminal-weight-contamination | c_0(q_L) | ≤ | $(1+\epsilon/10)Z$ | The feasible case and the stated terminal annealing parameter. |
| q-oracle-queries-per-state-rank | Original independence queries for a state rank | ≤ | $2n$ | One evaluation of the paired-matroid rank. |
| q-multiplier-bit-length | Bit length of multiplier numerators and denominators | = | $O\bigl(1+L\log(N_{\rm av}+1)\bigr)$ | All histories, including inaccurate estimates, before a zero-denominator abort. |
| q-output-rational-bit-length | Bit length of the rational product | = | $O\bigl(n+L\log(N_{\rm av}+1)+Ln\log(2n)\bigr)$ | Completed phases with positive transversal counts. |
| q-uniform-integer-call-cap | Uniform-integer calls in a run | ≤ | $3L(B_{\rm restart}+N_{\rm av})$ | All underlying transition attempts of one run. |
| q-bounded-run-success | Success probability of a bounded run | ≥ | $\frac{29}{32}$ | One run after the exact feasibility pretest. |
| q-amplification-run-count | J | = | $10b_\delta+1$ | The requested confidence parameter. |

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-defecttransfer",
          "exact_quote": "Assume the multipliers are good, and let $H:\\Omega\\to\\mathbb R$. For each defect type $ij$ there is an event $\\mathcal A\\subseteq\\Omega_0$ obtained by prescribing the selected elements in pairs $i,j$, with $\\mu(\\mathcal A)\\ge1/4$. Its conditional mean $\\overline H'=\\mathbb E_\\mu[H\\mid\\mathcal A]$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:defecttransfer}\n (\\overline H_{ij}-\\overline H')^2\n       \\le C_{\\rm d}\\frac{D(H)}{c_0},\n \\qquad C_{\\rm d}=8+32n,$$ and $$\\label{eq:conditionalmean}\n (\\overline H'-\\overline H_0)^2\\le4\\operatorname{Var}_\\mu H.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-114.2"
        }

            "anchor": "eq:good",
            "exact_quote": "Fix $q\\in(0,1]$. For each defect type $ij$, choose a positive multiplier $w_{ij}$, and define $$\\label{eq:chainweights}\n \\lambda(S)=\n \\begin{cases}\n f_q(S),&S\\in\\Omega_0,\\\\\n w_{ij}f_q(S),&S\\in\\Omega_{ij},\n \\end{cases}\n \\qquad\n \\Lambda=\\sum_{S\\in\\Omega}\\lambda(S),\\qquad \\pi(S)=\\frac{\\lambda(S)}\\Lambda.$$ The conditional law on transversals is $\\mu(S)=f_q(S)/c_0$ for $S\\in\\Omega_0$; it does not depend on the multipliers. Call the multipliers *good* if $$\\label{eq:good}\n \\frac14w^*_{ij}\\le w_{ij}\\le4w^*_{ij},\n \\qquad w^*_{ij}=\\frac{c_0}{c_{ij}}.$$ For good multipliers, $$\\label{eq:typemasses}\n \\Lambda\\le5n^2c_0,\\qquad\n \\pi(\\Omega_0)\\ge\\frac1{5n^2},\\qquad\n \\pi(\\Omega_{ij})\\ge\\frac1{20n^2}.$$ Indeed each defect has total multiplied weight between $c_0/4$ and $4c_0$, and there are $n(n-1)$ defects.",

          "exact_quote": "With good multipliers, $P_A$ is reversible with stationary law $\\mu$, irreducible, and lazy. Its inverse spectral gap is at most $18n^4$.           "source_id": "PM-114.2"
        }

    {
      "computational_model": null,
      "effectivity": "effective",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:minmu",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{eq:minmu}\n \\mu_j(S)\\ge\\frac{q_L^n}{2^n}\\ge2^{-n(L+1)}.$$ By [lem:trace](#lem:trace) and <a href=\"#eq:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:parameters\">[eq:parameters]</a>, after $\\tau$ trace transitions the density relative to the current $\\mu_a$ lies between $1/2$ and $3/2$, from any initial distribution. Indeed, $$\\exp\\bigl(-\\tau/(18n^4)\\bigr)\\,2^{n(L+1)}\\le\\tfrac12.$$ At the start of each stage the law is at most $4\\mu_a$: at the previous parameter it is at most twice that parameter’s transversal law, and <a href=\"#eq:mudrift\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mudrift\">[eq:mudrift]</a> costs at most a further factor of two. For the first stage the starting law is exactly $\\mu_0$. Pointwise domination by $4\\mu_a$ persists through the stage because $\\mu_a$ is stationary for its trace. Thus <a href=\"#eq:returncost\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:returncost\">[eq:returncost]</a>, applied at each trace step, implies $$\\label{eq:restartmean}\n \\mathbb E[\\text{underlying transitions in the restart}]\n \\le20n^2L\\tau.$$ Markov’s inequality yields $$\\label{eq:restartfailure}\n \\mathbb P[\\text{restart exceeds }B_{\\rm restart}]\n \\le\\frac{L}{100(L+1)^2}\\le\\frac1{32(L+1)}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-114.2"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-transversal-pointwise-floor",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "\\mu_j(S)",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "The annealed conditional transversal laws.",
        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "eq:schedulebounds",
            "exact_quote": "Bernoulli’s inequality and $1-x\\le e^{-x}$ give $$\\label{eq:schedulebounds}\n \\rho^n\\ge\\frac12,\\qquad\n \\rho^{2n}\\le e^{-1}<\\frac12,\\qquad\n 2^{-L}\\le q_L\\le2^{-(n+b)}.$$ For every state and every $j<L$, $$\\label{eq:stepweight}\n \\frac12\\le\\frac{f_{q_{j+1}}(S)}{f_{q_j}(S)}\n       =\\rho^{d(S)}\\le1.$$ The same inequalities hold for each ratio $c_T(q_{j+1})/c_T(q_j)$. Thus the ideal multiplier $w^*_{ij}=c_0/c_{ij}$ changes by a factor between $1/2$ and $2$ per phase. Write $\\mu_j$ for the transversal law at $q_j$. Equation <a href=\"#eq:stepweight\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:stepweight\">[eq:stepweight]</a> also gives $$\\label{eq:mudrift}\n \\mu_j\\le2\\mu_{j+1}.$$",
            "source_id": "PM-114.2"
          }
        ],
        "parameter_scope": "All scheduled phases and transversal states.",
        "quantifiers": [

            "source_id": "PM-114.2"
          }
        ]

      "value_latex": "2^{-n(L+1)}"
    },

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:averageMSE",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{eq:averageMSE}\n \\frac{40000n^8}{N_{\\rm av}}.$$ There are $n(n-1)+2\\le2n^2$ observables. Applying Markov’s inequality to each squared error and taking a union bound gives $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P\\bigl[\\text{some relative error exceeds }\\eta\\bigr]\n &\\le 2n^2\\frac{40000n^8}{N_{\\rm av}}\n                       \\frac{400n^4}{\\eta^2}\\notag\\\\\n &\\le\\frac{0.0032}{L+1}\\le\\frac1{32(L+1)}.\n \\label{eq:estimationfailure}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Both error probabilities were bounded in the uncapped experiment. Their union contains failure of the actual phase, since the two simulations agree whenever the restart finishes within its cap. When neither event occurs, all empirical type probabilities are positive and no zero-count abort occurs.",
          "source_id": "PM-114.2"
        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-paired-reduction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:reduction): Use a direct sum with the dual, identify common bases with transversal bases, test feasibility exactly, and introduce positive rank-deficiency weights. -> A positive-weight paired state space whose basis transversals encode the target count.
- **step-quadratic-signatures** [claim](#stmt-thm-bh) / [proof](#sec:algebra): Apply the Lorentzian rank-polynomial theorem and its closure properties to the selected-element and omitted-element generating polynomials. -> Quadratic signature properties for both generating polynomials.
- **step-defect-coefficient-inequalities** [claim](#stmt-lem-coefficient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coefficient): Condition on a partial transversal, extract occupancy coefficients, and use the Hessian determinant and Schur-complement constraints. -> The pair and triple inequalities for conditional defect totals.
- **step-balanced-signed-transport** [claim](#stmt-lem-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transport): Assign balanced signed demands recursively, bound splitting costs by omitted-element quadratic forms, conserve future-slot expressions, and route leaf demands through exchange cliques. -> An exchange flow with the required divergences and the stated transport-energy bound.
- **step-transversal-conditioning-variance** [claim](#stmt-lem-transversalvariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transversalvariance): Choose each conditioning pair through an acyclic score graph, apply transport at each branch, and sum variance decompositions using disjoint vertices at each depth. -> A bound for arbitrary variation on transversals.
- **step-defect-to-transversal-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-defecttransfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-defecttransfer): Select a frequent assignment in the distinguished pairs, reinterpret singleton and ordinary slots, apply transport, and use conditional Jensen bounds. -> Defect-class means controlled through a frequent transversal event.
- **step-observable-variance** [claim](#stmt-prop-observable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-observable): Combine transversal variance and defect-mean transfer, then normalize exchange energy using the stationary mass bounds. -> The partition-restricted observable Poincaré inequality.
- **step-warm-observable-averages** [claim](#stmt-lem-timeaverages) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-timeaverages): Use orthogonal projection and a resolvent energy bound in stationarity, sum nonnegative covariances, and dominate complete trajectory laws from a warm start. -> The empirical-average mean squared error bound.
- **step-transversal-trace** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace): Harmonically extend transversal functions, identify trace energy, derive the trace gap from transversal variance, and compute excursion cost through stationary occupations. -> Trace mixing and expected return-cost estimates.
- **step-annealing-and-learning** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:algorithm): Use fresh independent restarts through stored parameter levels, estimate type frequencies and annealing ratios, induct on multiplier accuracy, and control the telescoping product and terminal contamination. -> A constant-success approximation from exact rational random choices.
- **step-bounded-bit-amplification** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:implementation): Compute ranks through the input oracles, use exact integer accumulators and rational updates, cap all rejection trials and restart attempts, and amplify with the median of independent bounded runs. -> The every-execution polynomial-work approximation guarantee at the requested confidence.
- **step-independent-set-counting** [claim](#stmt-cor-intersection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-intersection): Find the maximum feasible cardinality exactly, truncate both matroids for prescribed-cardinality counting, and sum confidence-allocated estimates for unrestricted counting. -> Counting guarantees for prescribed, unrestricted, and maximum cardinalities, including unequal input ranks.
- **step-feasible-almost-uniform-self-reduction** [claim](#stmt-cor-intersection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-intersection): Construct residual minor oracles, test each branch exactly, estimate positive completion counts with fresh randomness, round branch probabilities dyadically, and couple decisions with the ideal uniform recursion. -> An almost-uniform sampler that always returns a feasible member of a nonempty requested family.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-paired-basis-correspondence**: A transversal of the paired direct-sum matroid is a basis exactly when its selected original indices form a basis of both input matroids.
- **invariant-oracle-rank-weight**: The paired rank can be evaluated through greedy rank computations in the original matroids; its rank deficiency determines the exact positive rational state weight.
- **invariant-observable-scope**: The observable Poincaré inequality controls the projection retaining individual transversal values and defect-class means. It does not establish a bound for arbitrary fluctuations inside a defect class.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-unpromised-oracles**: Apply the counting guarantee to arbitrary subset predicates or noisy independence answers. -> Greedy ranks, duality, and exact feasibility decisions require the promised matroid structure and exact answers.
- **anti-use-full-chain-gap**: Treat the observable Poincaré estimate as a spectral-gap bound for every observable on the enlarged chain. -> Within-defect fluctuations are removed by the projection and are outside the asserted variance control.
- **anti-use-adaptive-endpoint-start**: Reuse the previous estimation trajectory's endpoint as the next phase's warm start after conditioning on the learned multipliers. -> The observations reveal that endpoint, so conditioning on the phase history can destroy the required warm-start bound.
- **anti-use-uncapped-execution**: Implement exact rational choices or trace excursions with unrestricted retries while claiming polynomial work on every execution. -> The source's work guarantee depends on explicit transition and random-trial caps, including aborts on exceptional histories.
- **anti-use-exact-count-certificate**: Use an approximate count as an exact cardinality or integrity certificate. -> The counting output is a rational estimate with a probabilistic relative-error guarantee.
- **anti-use-finite-harness-as-fpras-proof**: Treat agreement of the finite paired-rank evaluator with its exhaustive oracle as verification of the complete approximation scheme. -> The harness checks paired ranks, rank-deficiency weights, and transversal-basis correspondence on its declared finite inputs. It does not execute annealing, learn multipliers, or verify the probabilistic counting and sampling guarantees.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite constructive target is the paired reduction and exact state-weight evaluation, rather than the complete randomized approximation scheme. The source states: Both original ranks are computed greedily. The independent oracle enumerates original bases and their dual complements to recover paired ranks directly from the resulting basis family.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with n, r, I1, I2, q, and states; subsets are integer bitmasks.
# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= n <= 5; I1 and I2 are complete exact independence families of equal-rank matroids; 0 < q <= 1; states have cardinality n on the paired ground set.
# OUTPUTS: One JSON row per state: paired rank, deficiency, weight numerator, weight denominator, transversal-basis flag.
# TIME: Polynomially many independence membership queries per state, plus exact rational arithmetic; reading the explicit oracle tables is additional work.
# SPACE: Input oracle tables, state masks, output rows, and exact rational integers.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer bitmasks and exact rational arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Malformed input or a detected violated precondition raises an exception; promised matroid axioms are independently checked by the oracle.
import json
import sys
from sympy import Rational


def greedy_rank(mask, family, n):
    chosen = 0
    for i in range(n):
        bit = 1 << i
        if mask & bit and (chosen | bit) in family:
            chosen |= bit
    return chosen.bit_count()


def main():
    obj = json.load(sys.stdin)
    n = obj['n']
    r = obj['r']
    assert type(n) is int and 0 <= n <= 5
    assert type(r) is int and 0 <= r <= n
    full = (1 << n) - 1
    families = []
    for key in ('I1', 'I2'):
        values = obj[key]
        assert isinstance(values, list)
        assert all(type(v) is int and 0 <= v <= full for v in values)
        assert len(values) == len(set(values)) and 0 in values
        families.append(set(values))
    I1, I2 = families
    assert greedy_rank(full, I1, n) == r
    assert greedy_rank(full, I2, n) == r
    q_values = obj['q']
    assert isinstance(q_values, list) and len(q_values) == 2
    assert all(type(v) is int for v in q_values)
    assert q_values[1] > 0
    q = Rational(q_values[0], q_values[1])
    assert 0 < q <= 1
    rows = []
    for state in obj['states']:
        assert type(state) is int and 0 <= state < (1 << (2 * n))
        assert state.bit_count() == n
        x = 0
        y = 0
        transversal = True
        for i in range(n):
            sx = (state >> (2 * i)) & 1
            sy = (state >> (2 * i + 1)) & 1
            x |= sx << i
            y |= sy << i
            transversal = transversal and sx + sy == 1
        rank = greedy_rank(x, I1, n) + y.bit_count() - r + greedy_rank(full ^ y, I2, n)
        deficiency = n - rank
        assert 0 <= deficiency <= n
        weight = q ** deficiency
        rows.append([rank, deficiency, int(weight.p), int(weight.q), bool(transversal and rank == n)])
    print(json.dumps(rows, separators=(',', ':')))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

1) & A\n        for B in independent:\n            if A.bit_count() < B.bit_count():\n                require(any((A | (1 << i)) in independent for i in range(n) if (B & (1 << i)) and not (A & (1 << i))))\n    maximal = [A for A in independent if not any(A != B and A & B == A for B in independent)]\n    require(bool(maximal))\n    require(all(A.bit_count() == r for A in maximal))\n    return maximal\n\n\ndef exact_rows(obj):\n    n = obj['n']\n    r = obj['r']\n    require(type(n) is int and 0 <= n <= 5)\n    require(type(r) is int and 0 <= r <= n)\n    full = (1 << n) - 1\n    bases1 = verified_bases(obj['I1'], n, r)\n    bases2 = verified_bases(obj['I2'], n, r)\n    qv = obj['q']\n    require(isinstance(qv, list) and len(qv) == 2)\n    require(all(type(v) is int for v in qv) and qv[1] > 0)\n    q = Fraction(qv[0], qv[1])\n    require(0 < q <= 1)\n    paired_bases = []\n    for A in bases1:\n        for B in bases2:\n            complement = full ^ B\n            paired = sum(1 << (2 * i) for i in range(n) if A & (1 << i))\n            paired |= sum(1 << (2 * i + 1) for i in range(n) if complement & (1 << i))\n            require(paired.bit_count() == n)\n            paired_bases.append(paired)\n    common = set(bases1) & set(bases2)\n    rows = []\n    for state in obj['states']:\n        require(type(state) is int and 0 <= state < (1 << (2 * n)))\n        require(state.bit_count() == n)\n        rank = max((state & basis).bit_count() for basis in paired_bases)\n        deficiency = n - rank\n        weight = Fraction(1)\n        for _ in range(deficiency):\n            weight *= q\n        transversal = all(((state >> (2 * i)) & 3) in (1, 2) for i in range(n))\n        selected = sum(1 << i for i in range(n) if state & (1 << (2 * i)))\n        is_common_basis = transversal and selected in common\n        require(is_common_basis == (transversal and state in paired_bases))\n        rows.append([rank, deficiency, weight.numerator, weight.denominator, is_common_basis])\n    return rows\n\n\ndef check_rows(value, wanted):\n    require(isinstance(value, list) and len(value) == len(wanted))\n    for row, target in zip(value, wanted):\n        require(isinstance(row, list) and len(row) == 5)\n        require(all(type(row[i]) is int for i in range(4)))\n        require(type(row[4]) is bool)\n        require(row == target)\n\n\ndef main():\n    with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n        obj = json.load(stream)\n    with open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n        actual = json.load(stream)\n    wanted = exact_rows(obj)\n    declared = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n    check_rows(declared, wanted)\n    check_rows(actual, wanted)\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    try:\n        main()\n    except Exception:\n        sys.exit(1)\n",
    "output_contract": "A JSON array in input-state order. Each row is [paired_rank, rank_deficiency, exact_weight_numerator, exact_weight_denominator, transversal_basis]. Rational weights use reduced numerator-denominator form. The Boolean is true exactly when the state contains one element per pair and is a basis of the paired direct sum. This output is not an approximate count.",
    "reason": "The finite constructive target is the paired reduction and exact state-weight evaluation, rather than the complete randomized approximation scheme. The source states: Both original ranks are computed greedily. The independent oracle enumerates original bases and their dual complements to recover paired ranks directly from the resulting basis family.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON with n, r, I1, I2, q, and states; subsets are integer bitmasks.\n# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= n <= 5; I1 and I2 are complete exact independence families of equal-rank matroids; 0 < q <= 1; states have cardinality n on the paired ground set.\n# OUTPUTS: One JSON row per state: paired rank, deficiency, weight numerator, weight denominator, transversal-basis flag.\n# TIME: Polynomially many independence membership queries per state, plus exact rational arithmetic; reading the explicit oracle tables is additional work.\n# SPACE: Input oracle tables, state masks, output rows, and exact rational integers.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer bitmasks and exact rational arithmetic.\n# FAILURE: Malformed input or a detected violated precondition raises an exception; promised matroid axioms are independently checked by the oracle.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom sympy import Rational\n\n\ndef greedy_rank(mask, family, n):\n    chosen = 0\n    for i in range(n):\n        bit = 1 << i\n        if mask & bit and (chosen | bit) in family:\n            chosen |= bit\n    return chosen.bit_count()\n\n\ndef main():\n    obj = json.load(sys.stdin)\n    n = obj['n']\n    r = obj['r']\n    assert type(n) is int and 0 <= n <= 5\n    assert type(r) is int and 0 <= r <= n\n    full = (1 << n) - 1\n    families = []\n    for key in ('I1', 'I2'):\n        values = obj[key]\n        assert isinstance(values, list)\n        assert all(type(v) is int and 0 <= v <= full for v in values)\n        assert len(values) == len(set(values)) and 0 in values\n        families.append(set(values))\n    I1, I2 = families\n    assert greedy_rank(full, I1, n) == r\n    assert greedy_rank(full, I2, n) == r\n    q_values = obj['q']\n    assert isinstance(q_values, list) and len(q_values) == 2\n    assert all(type(v) is int for v in q_values)\n    assert q_values[1] > 0\n    q = Rational(q_values[0], q_values[1])\n    assert 0 < q <= 1\n    rows = []\n    for state in obj['states']:\n        assert type(state) is int and 0 <= state < (1 << (2 * n))\n        assert state.bit_count() == n\n        x = 0\n        y = 0\n        transversal = True\n        for i in range(n):\n            sx = (state >> (2 * i)) & 1\n            sy = (state >> (2 * i + 1)) & 1\n            x |= sx << i\n            y |= sy << i\n            transversal = transversal and sx + sy == 1\n        rank = greedy_rank(x, I1, n) + y.bit_count() - r + greedy_rank(full ^ y, I2, n)\n        deficiency = n - rank\n        assert 0 <= deficiency <= n\n        weight = q ** deficiency\n        rows.append([rank, deficiency, int(weight.p), int(weight.q), bool(transversal and rank == n)])\n    print(json.dumps(rows, separators=(',', ':')))\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "[[0,0,1,1,true]]",
        "input": "{\"n\":0,\"r\":0,\"I1\":[0],\"I2\":[0],\"q\":[1,2],\"states\":[0]}"
      },
      {
        "expected": "[[0,1,1,2,false],[1,0,1,1,true]]",
        "input": "{\"n\":1,\"r\":0,\"I1\":[0],\"I2\":[0],\"q\":[1,2],\"states\":[1,2]}"

        "input": "{\"n\":2,\"r\":1,\"I1\":[0,1,2],\"I2\":[0,1,2],\"q\":[1,2],\"states\":[3,5,6,9,10,12]}"

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The paper supplies a randomized counting procedure for common bases with bounded oracle calls and bit operations on every execution. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sampler can generate common independent sets, but the paper provides no construction relating game states, physical trajectories, or simulation dynamics to these families. |
| tools | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The state-weight evaluator is a concrete reusable component: it computes the paired matroid rank from the original independence oracles and constructs each weight from rank deficiency. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The construction combines annealing, learned defect multipliers, trace restarts, capped rational choices, and confidence amplification into an oracle approximation algorithm. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The implementation explicitly represents states by bit indicators, stores unreduced rational multipliers, and accumulates ratio observations with a common integer denominator. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The implementation contract bounds oracle queries and bit operations but specifies no accelerator architecture, parallel arithmetic construction, or hardware cost model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Unbiased bits implement uniform integer choices and rational acceptance probabilities; the paper supplies no cryptographic primitive, adversarial security guarantee, or integrity construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The learning step estimates defect multipliers from chain observations. It does not construct a predictive model, training objective, or statistical learning guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Bipartite assignments admit the stated partition-matroid construction. Their perfect matchings are common bases, and common independent-set counting and sampling extend the construction to prescribed or maximum matching cardinality. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Independent restarts and repeated runs are probabilistic components of a centralized oracle algorithm; no distributed protocol or construction for communication, failures, or shared-state coordination is supplied. |
| other (Combinatorial sampling) | True | strong | implementable | inferred | Exact branch-feasibility tests, residual matroid minors, approximate completion counts, and dyadic branch probabilities construct an almost-uniform sampler that always returns a feasible member when the family is nonempty. |

After conditioning on that history, the endpoint need not satisfy the polynomial warm-start bound needed below. Each phase therefore generates a fresh starting transversal from the known law $\\mu_0$, through the stored parameter levels and with independent randomness.\n\nFor $j=0,\\ldots,L-1$, perform the following steps. Any abort mentioned below returns zero from the run.\n\n1.  **Restart to obtain a warm transversal.** Start from a uniform transversal, using $n$ fair bits. For $a=1,\\ldots,j$, perform $\\tau$ trace transitions at parameter $q_a$ with the stored multipliers $w^{(a)}$. Each trace transition is implemented by running the underlying Metropolis chain until the next visit to $\\Omega_0$, at a strictly positive time. The endpoint of one stage is the starting transversal of the next. Before attempting an underlying transition, reserve one unit from a budget of $B_{\\rm restart}$ for this entire restart; if no unit remains, abort before drawing any bits for that transition. For $j=0$, simply use the initial uniform transversal. <a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-promised-matroid-axioms** (hypothesis): The independence predicates must describe genuine matroids. Heredity alone does not supply the augmentation needed by greedy rank computations, duality, and matroid intersection. Blocked use: ['Apply the algorithm directly to arbitrary hereditary feasibility predicates.', 'Treat general budget or scheduling constraints as matroids without establishing augmentation.']
- **limit-finite-exact-oracle-interface** (hypothesis): The input ground set must be explicitly enumerated and the independence oracles fixed and exact. The query and bit-work guarantees do not establish correctness for noisy, changing, or infinite independence systems. Blocked use: ['Replace exact independence answers with predictions or approximate feasibility tests.', 'Apply the finite subset-query algorithm to an infinite ground set.']
- **limit-equal-rank-common-bases** (formal_scope): The direct common-basis theorem assumes equal ranks and the stated rational accuracy and confidence parameters. Unequal ranks are handled for common independent sets through the separate truncation and self-reduction argument. Blocked use: ['Apply the paired common-basis construction with unequal ranks while retaining its rank and basis correspondence.', "Use accuracy or confidence parameters outside the theorem's stated range."]
- **limit-feasibility-before-annealing** (edge_case): The empty-ground-set case and infeasible common-basis instances are resolved before annealing. The terminal relative-contamination argument uses the positive count established by the feasibility test. Blocked use: ['Run the positive-count contamination argument on an instance with no common basis.', 'Evaluate the annealing schedule on an empty ground set without the initial special case.']
- **limit-positive-rank-weights** (hypothesis): The rank-deficiency construction retains strictly positive weights throughout the prescribed parameter range. Setting the parameter to zero would remove the positivity used by the exchange and transport arguments. Blocked use: ['Replace the terminal positive parameter by zero while invoking the same connectivity and transport proof.', 'Use rank weights outside the positive range covered by the Lorentzian theorem.']
- **limit-transport-slot-contract** (hypothesis): Transport applies to the specified partition into included elements, excluded elements, singleton slots, and ordinary slots, with the required cardinality identity and positive slot multipliers. The doubled-slot totals are auxiliary totals and need not represent vertices of the transport graph. Blocked use: ['Route exchanges through arbitrary occupancy patterns without checking the slot contract.', 'Treat every set contributing to a doubled-slot total as an allowed transport vertex.']
- **limit-good-multiplier-regime** (hypothesis): The transversal variance, defect transfer, observable averages, and trace estimates require good multipliers. Arbitrary positive multipliers provide neither the stated type-mass lower bounds nor the quantitative simulation guarantees. Blocked use: ['Invoke the polynomial variance and trace estimates for arbitrary positive defect multipliers.', 'Assume inaccurate learned weights remain good without the successful-history induction.']
- **limit-projected-observable-scope** (formal_scope): The observable Poincaré inequality controls the block-averaged function, retaining individual transversal values and defect-class means. It does not supply a variance bound for arbitrary variation within a defect class. Blocked use: ['Infer rapid mixing of the full enlarged chain from the projected variance inequality.', 'Apply the time-average lemma to an observable that is not fixed by the projection.']
- **limit-conditioned-adaptive-endpoint** (hypothesis): After conditioning on observations used to learn multipliers, the observed trajectory endpoint need not be a valid warm start. Fresh independent restarts from the known initial law are part of the argument. Blocked use: ['Reuse an observed phase endpoint as a certified warm start after conditioning on its estimates.', 'Correlate restart randomness with the earlier phase history while retaining the same domination argument.']
- **limit-expected-trace-cost** (formal_scope): The trace excursion estimate bounds an expected cost under warm-start domination. The every-execution work bound additionally requires the explicit restart-attempt cap. Blocked use: ['Interpret the trace return-cost estimate as a deterministic bound on every excursion.', 'Remove restart reservations and claim bounded work on exceptional trajectories.']
- **limit-annealing-schedule-and-ratio-aborts** (hypothesis): The stated schedule, observation budget, and multiplier update are linked by the phase-error analysis. Zero empirical type probabilities cause an abort; they are not safe denominators or exact evidence of an empty type. Blocked use: ['Divide by a zero empirical type count.', 'Shorten observation phases or change the annealing schedule while claiming the unchanged relative-error guarantee.', 'Use a zero observed frequency to delete a positive-weight defect class.']
- **limit-exact-state-weight-arithmetic** (hypothesis): State weights and ratios are evaluated using original-oracle rank computations and exact integer or rational arithmetic. The proof does not certify a floating-point replacement or an incorrect dual-rank query. Blocked use: ['Replace exact accumulators and rational comparisons with unchecked floating-point arithmetic.', 'Query the selected second-copy subset where the dual-rank identity requires its complement.', 'Use polynomial evaluations as though they were supplied by independence oracles.']
- **limit-bounded-random-choice-trials** (hypothesis): Uniform-integer rejection sampling is capped, and exceeding the trial cap aborts the run. Unrestricted retries preserve exactness of an individual choice but do not establish the every-execution work guarantee. Blocked use: ['Use an unbounded rejection loop while claiming polynomial work on every execution.', 'Ignore bit-cap aborts in the success-probability accounting.']
- **limit-independent-confidence-amplification** (hypothesis): The confidence amplification uses independent bounded runs and their rational median. Reusing correlated runs does not justify the product-moment argument used to bound failure. Blocked use: ['Treat repeated estimates generated with shared randomness as independent amplification runs.', 'Replace the rational median with an arbitrary aggregate while invoking the median guarantee.']
- **limit-approximation-not-exact-counting** (approximation): The general counting output is a nonnegative rational with a probabilistic relative-error guarantee. Exact emptiness does not imply exact cardinality on feasible instances. Blocked use: ['Publish the returned rational as an exact general count.', 'Use the approximation as an exact integrity or cardinality certificate.']
- **limit-sampling-exact-branch-feasibility** (hypothesis): Sampling keeps included elements common independent and decides branch emptiness exactly. A zero randomized estimate on an inaccurate run is not proof that the corresponding branch is empty. Blocked use: ['Discard a feasible branch because its randomized count estimate is zero.', 'Construct an inclusion minor without first checking independence of the enlarged included set.', 'Change the original target cardinality during prescribed-cardinality self-reduction.']
- **limit-sampling-distributional-tolerance** (approximation): The sampler guarantees feasibility on every execution for a nonempty family, but its distribution is only almost uniform within the stated total-variation tolerance. Blocked use: ['Claim exact uniform sampling from the approximate completion-count recursion.', 'Interpret always-feasible output as exact agreement with the uniform distribution.']
- **limit-proof-devices-not-enumeration-algorithm** (formal_scope): Partition sums, conditioning trees, and transport flows are analytical devices. Their finite mathematical construction does not make exhaustive evaluation of them part of the polynomial oracle algorithm. Blocked use: ["Implement the approximation scheme by explicitly enumerating the proof's partition sums or conditioning tree.", 'Charge no work for computing analytical flows that the algorithm does not compute.']
- **limit-finite-panel-coverage** (formal_scope): Finite checks of paired ranks, weights, and basis correspondence do not verify the complete approximation scheme, its adaptive simulation analysis, or its counting and sampling guarantees. Execution receipts for those checks are ABSENT from the supplied results. Blocked use: ['Treat partial finite reduction checks as a certificate of the main randomized counting theorem.', 'Report successful mutant execution or host certification before receiving the corresponding receipts.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 114 (`lean/docs/114.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Approximate counting of common bases of two matroids](../../preprints/Approximate-counting-of-common-bases-of-two-matroids-September-23-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result gives a fully polynomial randomized approximation scheme for the number of common bases of two equal-rank matroids on an enumerated finite ground set, using independence oracles. For rational $0<\varepsilon,\delta<1$, the nonnegative rational output has relative error at most $\varepsilon$ with probability at least $1-\delta$. A zero count produces zero on every execution. Oracle calls and bit operations have polynomial bounds on every random tape in the input size, $\varepsilon^{-1}$, and $\log(\delta^{-1})$.

Comparator source `CommonBasesFPRAS.lean` (4848 bytes, sha256 `f20d91f7fe7f1cccee71d1312754a258c0b4f69925be9485ba80957b76d09f95`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace CommonBasesFPRAS

abbrev IndependenceOracle (n : ℕ) := Bool → (Fin n → Bool) → Bool

def ExactOracles {n : ℕ} (M₁ M₂ : Matroid (Fin n))
    (O : IndependenceOracle n) : Prop :=
  ∀ (which : Bool) (A : Fin n → Bool),
    O which A = true ↔ (if which then M₂ else M₁).Indep {i | A i = true}

noncomputable def commonBaseCount {n : ℕ} (M₁ M₂ : Matroid (Fin n)) : ℕ :=
  Nat.card {A : Finset (Fin n) // M₁.IsBase (A : Set (Fin n)) ∧
    M₂.IsBase (A : Set (Fin n))}

inductive Instruction (k s : ℕ) where
  | push (register : Fin (k + 8)) (bit : Bool) (next : Fin (s + 1))
  | pop (register : Fin (k + 8)) (empty zero one : Fin (s + 1))
  | coin (zero one : Fin (s + 1))
  | query (which : Bool) (register : Fin (k + 8)) (no yes : Fin (s + 1))
  | halt

def Program (k s : ℕ) := Fin (s + 1) → Instruction k s

def binaryValue : List Bool → ℕ
  | [] => 0
  | b :: l => (if b then 1 else 0) + 2 * binaryValue l

structure MachineConfig (k s : ℕ) where
  registers : Fin (k + 8) → List Bool
  control : Fin (s + 1)
  output : Option ℚ := none
  oracleCalls : ℕ := 0
  bitOperations : ℕ := 0

def inputLength (n r : ℕ) (ε δ : ℚ) : ℕ :=
  6 + n.bits.length + r.bits.length + ε.num.natAbs.bits.length + ε.den.bits.length +
    δ.num.natAbs.bits.length + δ.den.bits.length

def initialConfig (k s n r : ℕ) (ε δ : ℚ) : MachineConfig k s where
  registers := fun i => match i.val with
    | 0 => n.bits
    | 1 => r.bits
    | 2 => ε.num.natAbs.bits
    | 3 => ε.den.bits
    | 4 => δ.num.natAbs.bits
    | 5 => δ.den.bits
    | _ => []
  control := 0

def machineStep {k s n : ℕ} (P : Program k s) (O : IndependenceOracle n)
    (randomBit : Bool) (c : MachineConfig k s) : MachineConfig k s :=
  if c.output.isSome then c else
  match P c.control with
  | .push i b next =>
    { c with
      registers := Function.update c.registers i (b :: c.registers i)
      control := next
      bitOperations := c.bitOperations + 1 }
  | .pop i empty zero one =>
    match c.registers i with
    | [] => { c with
      control := empty
      bitOperations := c.bitOperations + 1 }
    | b :: l =>
      { c with
      registers := Function.update c.registers i l
      control := if b then one else zero
      bitOperations := c.bitOperations + 1 }
  | .coin zero one =>
    { c with
      control := if randomBit then one else zero
      bitOperations := c.bitOperations + 1 }
  | .query which i no yes =>
    if (c.registers i).length = n then
      { c with
      control := if O which (fun j => (c.registers i)[j.val]?.getD false)
          then yes else no
      oracleCalls := c.oracleCalls + 1
      bitOperations := c.bitOperations + (c.registers i).length + n + 1 }
    else
      { c with
      output := some 0
      bitOperations := c.bitOperations + (c.registers i).length + n + 1 }
  | .halt =>
    { c with
      output := some ((binaryValue (c.registers ⟨6, by omega⟩) : ℚ) /
        (binaryValue (c.registers ⟨7, by omega⟩) : ℚ))
      bitOperations := c.bitOperations + (c.registers ⟨6, by omega⟩).length +
        (c.registers ⟨7, by omega⟩).length + 1 }

def execution {k s n : ℕ} (P : Program k s) (O : IndependenceOracle n)
    (c : MachineConfig k s) (bits : ℕ → Bool) : ℕ → MachineConfig k s
  | 0 => c
  | t + 1 => machineStep P O (bits t) (execution P O c bits t)

def finiteBits {B : ℕ} (bits : Fin B → Bool) (t : ℕ) : Bool :=
  if h : t < B then bits ⟨t, h⟩ else false

def relativeAccuracy (ε : ℚ) (Z : ℕ) (z : ℚ) : Prop :=
  (1-ε)*(Z:ℚ)≤z ∧ z≤(1+ε)*(Z:ℚ)

def resourceBound (C d n r : ℕ) (ε δ : ℚ) : ℕ :=
  C * (1 + n + inputLength n r ε δ + ⌈ε⁻¹⌉₊ + Nat.clog 2 ⌈δ⁻¹⌉₊) ^ d

def MainTheorem : Prop :=
  ∃ (k s : ℕ) (P : Program k s) (C d : ℕ), 0 < C ∧
    ∀ (n r : ℕ) (M₁ M₂ : Matroid (Fin n)) (O : IndependenceOracle n)
      (ε δ : ℚ),
      M₁.E = Set.univ → M₂.E = Set.univ →
      M₁.eRank = (r : ℕ∞) → M₂.eRank = (r : ℕ∞) →
      ExactOracles M₁ M₂ O → 0 < ε → ε < 1 → 0 < δ → δ < 1 →
      let B := resourceBound C d n r ε δ
      let Z := commonBaseCount M₁ M₂
      (∀ bits : ℕ → Bool,
        let out := execution P O (initialConfig k s n r ε δ) bits B
        out.output.isSome ∧ out.oracleCalls ≤ B ∧ out.bitOperations ≤ B ∧
        ∀ z, out.output = some z → 0 ≤ z ∧ (Z = 0 → z = 0)) ∧
      (1 - δ) * (2 : ℚ) ^ B ≤
        (Nat.card {bits : Fin B → Bool //
          ∃ z, (execution P O (initialConfig k s n r ε δ) (finiteBits bits) B).output
            = some z ∧ relativeAccuracy ε Z z} : ℚ)

end CommonBasesFPRAS

theorem common_bases_fpras : CommonBasesFPRAS.MainTheorem := by
  sorry

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give a fully polynomial randomized approximation scheme for counting the common bases of two arbitrary matroids of the same rank, supplied by independence oracles. The algorithm requires no explicit representation of either matroid and has polynomial bounds on both oracle calls and bit operations on every execution.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | There is a single randomized oracle algorithm with the following guarantee. |
| [stmt-thm-bh](#stmt-thm-bh) | theorem | `thm:BH` | 2 | For a matroid $N$ on a ground set $E$ and |
| [stmt-lem-coefficient](#stmt-lem-coefficient) | lemma | `lem:coefficient` | 3 | For distinct unassigned indices $i,j$, |
| [stmt-lem-transport](#stmt-lem-transport) | lemma | `lem:transport` | 4 | With the notation above, |
| [stmt-lem-transversalvariance](#stmt-lem-transversalvariance) | lemma | `lem:transversalvariance` | 5 | For good multipliers and every $H:\Omega\to\mathbb R$, |
| [stmt-lem-defecttransfer](#stmt-lem-defecttransfer) | lemma | `lem:defecttransfer` | 6 | Assume the multipliers are good, and let $H:\Omega\to\mathbb R$. |
| [stmt-prop-observable](#stmt-prop-observable) | proposition | `prop:observable` | 7 | For good multipliers and every $H:\Omega\to\mathbb R$, |
